I figured maybe I should talk to someone before it gets more out of hand.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear specifically about driving over high bridges or is it also about being in high places in general?\n\nPatient: It’s mainly the high bridges, especially the ones over the bay. I mean, I know they’re built fine and plenty of people drive over them every day, so I feel a little embarrassed saying it… but when I get close to one, my mind kind of jumps to, what if the car went right over the side? Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt anxious or uncomfortable when you're in a high place, but not necessarily driving, like being on a tall building or a mountain?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I mean, I can be up somewhere high and maybe feel a little aware of it, but the thing that really gets me is those bridges over the bay. I know they’re built fine and everyone drives them all the time, but when I get near one, my head goes to this thought like… what if the car went right over the side. It sounds dramatic, I know, but that’s the part that sticks with me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're a passenger in a car driving over a high bridge, or is your fear mostly related to when you're the one driving?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly when I’m the one driving, I think. I mean, even as a passenger I’m not thrilled about it, but when I’m behind the wheel and we’re coming up on one of those bay bridges, my mind just goes to this picture like the car could somehow go right over the side. I know that’s not likely, and I know people drive them every day, but… yeah, that’s the thought that gets me.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about driving over high bridges decrease if you were driving over a bridge of similar height, but not over water?\n\nPatient: I think it would probably be less, yeah. Not gone completely maybe, but the bay part is what really gets in my head. I know those bridges are safe and people drive them every day, but when I’m coming up on one over the water, my mind goes right to, like, what if the car somehow went over the side. It sounds silly when I say it, but that’s the thought.\n\nDoctor: Does the thought of losing control of the car occur to you in other driving situations, or is it pretty much limited to when you're approaching a high bridge?\n\nPatient: It’s pretty much just when I’m coming up on one of those high bridges, especially over the bay. I mean, I drive other places and I don’t really sit there thinking I’m going to lose control. I know that probably sounds over the top.\n\nDoctor: Does the fear of the car going over the side occur to you when you're stopped at the edge of a high bridge, or is it more related to the act of driving over it?\n\nPatient: It’s more when I’m actually driving over it, or even getting close to driving onto it. I don’t really think of it like being stopped there so much. I mean, I know they’re built fine and plenty of people drive over them every day, so I feel a little embarrassed saying it… but when I get close to one, my mind kind of jumps to, what if the car went right over the side? Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt anxious or uncomfortable when you're in a high place, but not necessarily driving, like being on a tall building or a mountain?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I mean, I can be up somewhere high and maybe feel a little aware of it, but the thing that really gets me is those bridges over the bay. I know they’re built fine and everyone drives them all the time, but when I get near one, my head goes to this thought like… what if the car went right over the side. It sounds dramatic, I know, but that’s the part that sticks with me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're a passenger in a car driving over a high bridge, or is your fear mostly related to when you're the one driving?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly when I’m the one driving, I think. I mean, even as a passenger I’m not thrilled about it, but when I’m behind the wheel and we’re coming up on one of those bay bridges, my mind just goes to this picture like the car could somehow go right over the side. I know that’s not likely, and I know people drive them every day, but… yeah, that’s the thought that gets me.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about driving over high bridges decrease if you were driving over a bridge of similar height, but not over water?\n\nPatient: I think it would probably be less, yeah. Not gone completely maybe, but the bay part is what really gets in my head. It sounds dramatic, I know, but that’s the part that sticks with me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're a passenger in a car driving over a high bridge, or is your fear mostly related to when you're the one driving?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly when I’m the one driving, I think. I mean, even as a passenger I’m not thrilled about it, but when I’m behind the wheel and we’re coming up on one of those bay bridges, my mind just goes to this picture like the car could somehow go right over the side. I know that’s not likely, and I know people drive them every day, but… yeah, that’s the thought that gets me.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about driving over high bridges decrease if you were driving over a bridge of similar height, but not over water?\n\nPatient: I think it would probably be less, yeah. Not gone completely maybe, but the bay part is what really gets in my head. I know that probably sounds over the top.\n\nDoctor: Does the fear of the car going over the side occur to you when you're stopped at the edge of a high bridge, or is it more related to the act of driving over it?\n\nPatient: It’s more when I’m actually driving over it, or even getting close to driving onto it. I don’t really think of it like being stopped there so much. That’s the part that bothers me.\n\nDoctor: Does this fear of losing control occur to you when you're driving in other situations that might make you feel a bit uneasy, like in heavy traffic or on winding roads?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I’m sort of planning my days around avoiding them, and if I can’t avoid one, I get through it, but I’m gripping the wheel pretty tight the whole way. I figured maybe I should talk to someone before it gets more out of hand.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear specifically about driving over high bridges or is it also about being in high places in general?\n\nPatient: It’s mainly the high bridges, especially the ones over the bay. I mean, I know they’re built fine and plenty of people drive over them every day, so I feel a little embarrassed saying it… but when I get close to one, my mind kind of jumps to, what if the car went right over the side? Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt anxious or uncomfortable when you're in a high place, but not necessarily driving, like being on a tall building or a mountain?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I mean, I can be up somewhere high and maybe feel a little aware of it, but the thing that really gets me is those bridges over the bay. I know they’re built fine and everyone drives them all the time, but when I get near one, my head goes to this thought like… what if the car went right over the side. It sounds dramatic, I know, but that’s the part that sticks with me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're a passenger in a car driving over a high bridge, or is your fear mostly related to when you're the one driving?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly when I’m the one driving, I think. I mean, even as a passenger I’m not thrilled about it, but when I’m behind the wheel and we’re coming up on one of those bay bridges, my mind just goes to this picture like the car could somehow go right over the side. I know that’s not likely, and I know people drive them every day, but… yeah, that’s the thought that gets me.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about driving over high bridges decrease if you were driving over a bridge of similar height, but not over water?\n\nPatient: I think it would probably be less, yeah. Not gone completely maybe, but the bay part is what really gets in my head. I know those bridges are safe and people drive them every day, but when I’m coming up on one over the water, my mind goes right to, like, what if the car somehow went over the side. It sounds silly when I say it, but that’s the thought.\n\nDoctor: Does the thought of losing control of the car occur to you in other driving situations, or is it pretty much limited to when you're approaching a high bridge?\n\nPatient: It’s pretty much just when I’m coming up on one of those high bridges, especially over the bay. I mean, I drive other places and I don’t really sit there thinking I’m going to lose control. But with those bridges… even though I know they’re built fine and people drive them every day, my mind kind of jumps to this idea that the car could go right over the side. I know that probably sounds over the top.\n\nDoctor: Does the fear of the car going over the side occur to you when you're stopped at the edge of a high bridge, or is it more related to the act of driving over it?\n\nPatient: It’s more when I’m actually driving over it, or even getting close to driving onto it. I don’t really think of it like being stopped there so much. I know those bay bridges are built fine and people go over them all day long, but when I’m coming up on one, my mind kind of acts like the car could go right over the side. I know that probably doesn’t make a lot of sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety would be less if you couldn't see the water below while driving over the bridge?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it probably would be less if I couldn’t see the water. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I know those bridges over the bay are built fine and people drive them every day. But when I get near one, my mind kind of goes to this thought that the car could go right over the side. The water being right there underneath makes that harder to shake, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety more about the act of driving over the high bridge itself, or about the potential consequences of something going wrong while driving over it?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more the consequences, if that makes sense. Like, I know those bridges over the bay are built fine, and plenty of folks drive over them every day with no problem. But when I get near one, my mind sort of jumps to, what if the car goes right over the side. I know that sounds a bit extreme, but that’s the part that gets to me.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your anxiety is specifically about the fear of the car going over the side of the bridge, or is it also about the general feeling of being in a situation where you might lose control?\n\nPatient: I’d say it’s mostly that specific thought, the car going over the side. I mean, I know the bridges over the bay are built fine, and people drive them every day, so I don’t want to make it sound like I think they’re unsafe. But when I get near one, my mind just kind of goes there, like the car could somehow go over. That’s the part that bothers me.\n\nDoctor: Does this fear of losing control occur to you when you're driving in other situations that might make you feel a bit uneasy, like in heavy traffic or on winding roads?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I mean, heavy traffic or a curvy road can make anybody a little tense, I guess, but that specific thought doesn’t really hit me there. It’s more when I’m coming up on those bridges over the bay. I know they’re built fine and people drive them every day, but my mind still acts like the car could go right over the side. I know that probably sounds a little silly, but that’s what comes up.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced similar anxiety or fears in situations other than driving over high bridges?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… at least not that I can think of. It’s pretty much just those high bridges over the bay. I know they’re built fine, and plenty of folks go over them every day, so I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. But when I get near one, my mind just jumps to the car going over the side. That’s the part that gets me.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else about driving over high bridges, other than the fear of the car going over the side, that contributes to your anxiety?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so… I mean, not really anything else I can put my finger on. I know those bridges over the bay are built fine, and folks drive them every day. It’s just when I get near one, my mind kind of acts like the car could go right over the side. That’s really the main thing, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced this fear of the car going over the side in any other type of bridge or overpass that isn't over water?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. At least, not that I’ve noticed. It’s really those bridges over the bay that set that off for me. I know they’re built fine and everybody drives them, so I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is, but when I get near one, my mind just goes to the car maybe going over the side.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I mean, I know the bridges over the bay are built fine, and people drive them every day, so I don’t want to make it sound like I think they’re unsafe. But when I get near one, my mind just kind of goes there, like the car could somehow go over. That’s the part that bothers me.\n\nDoctor: Does this fear of losing control occur to you when you're driving in other situations that might make you feel a bit uneasy, like in heavy traffic or on winding roads?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. It sounds silly when I say it, but that’s the thought.\n\nDoctor: Does the thought of losing control of the car occur to you in other driving situations, or is it pretty much limited to when you're approaching a high bridge?\n\nPatient: It’s pretty much just when I’m coming up on one of those high bridges, especially over the bay. I mean, I drive other places and I don’t really sit there thinking I’m going to lose control. I know that probably sounds over the top.\n\nDoctor: Does the fear of the car going over the side occur to you when you're stopped at the edge of a high bridge, or is it more related to the act of driving over it?\n\nPatient: It’s more when I’m actually driving over it, or even getting close to driving onto it. I know those bay bridges are built fine and people go over them all day long, but when I’m coming up on one, my mind kind of acts like the car could go right over the side. I know that probably doesn’t make a lot of sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety would be less if you couldn't see the water below while driving over the bridge?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it probably would be less if I couldn’t see the water. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I know those bridges over the bay are built fine and people drive them every day. But when I get near one, my mind kind of goes to this thought that the car could go right over the side. Like, I know those bridges over the bay are built fine, and plenty of folks drive over them every day with no problem. But when I get near one, my mind sort of jumps to, what if the car goes right over the side. I know that sounds a bit extreme, but that’s the part that gets to me.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your anxiety is specifically about the fear of the car going over the side of the bridge, or is it also about the general feeling of being in a situation where you might lose control?\n\nPatient: I’d say it’s mostly that specific thought, the car going over the side. I mean, I know the bridges over the bay are built fine, and people drive them every day, so I don’t want to make it sound like I think they’re unsafe. But when I get near one, my mind just kind of goes there, like the car could somehow go over. That’s the part that bothers me.\n\nDoctor: Does this fear of losing control occur to you when you're driving in other situations that might make you feel a bit uneasy, like in heavy traffic or on winding roads?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I mean, heavy traffic or a curvy road can make anybody a little tense, I guess, but that specific thought doesn’t really hit me there. It’s more when I’m coming up on those bridges over the bay. I know they’re built fine and people drive them every day, but my mind still acts like the car could go right over the side. I know that probably sounds a little silly, but that’s what comes up.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced similar anxiety or fears in situations other than driving over high bridges?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… at least not that I can think of. But when I get near one, my mind just jumps to the car going over the side. That’s the part that gets me.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else about driving over high bridges, other than the fear of the car going over the side, that contributes to your anxiety?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so… I mean, not really anything else I can put my finger on. I know those bridges over the bay are built fine, and folks drive them every day. It’s just when I get near one, my mind kind of acts like the car could go right over the side. That’s really the main thing, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced this fear of the car going over the side in any other type of bridge or overpass that isn't over water?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. At least, not that I’ve noticed. It’s really those bridges over the bay that set that off for me. I know they’re built fine and everybody drives them, so I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is, but when I get near one, my mind just goes to the car maybe going over the side.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety be the same if you were driving over a bridge of similar height, but over land?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it would be quite the same, honestly. I know those bridges are safe and everybody drives over them all the time, but when I’m coming up on one over the water, my brain just goes to, you know, the car going over the side. But when I get near one, my mind just kind of goes there, like the car could somehow go over. That’s the part that bothers me.\n\nDoctor: Does this fear of losing control occur to you when you're driving in other situations that might make you feel a bit uneasy, like in heavy traffic or on winding roads?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I know they’re built fine and people drive them every day, but my mind still acts like the car could go right over the side. I know that probably sounds a little silly, but that’s what comes up.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced similar anxiety or fears in situations other than driving over high bridges?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… at least not that I can think of.
